French team: Malo Gusto’s nightmarish evening!

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

For several gatherings, the corridors have been a real construction site for the French team. And it’s not this evening in Milan that’s going to fix things. At San Siro, the Blues started their meeting well, before gradually losing their footing and conceding their first defeat of the Zinedine Zidane era (3-1), on the occasion of this 5th day of the Nations League. In this debacle, Malo Gusto particularly suffered. Aligned in the right lane, the Chelsea full-back increased his attempts, struggling to contain the Italian offensives.

The former Lyonnais, however, had a great opportunity to get back on his feet, after a complicated start to the club season. Far from being an indisputable starter this season under Xabi Alonso, he was still called up by ZZ to compensate for Jules Koundé’s injury. “ He was the next player on my list. He knows the selection. He certainly has less playing time at the moment, but he already has experience at this level. This is therefore the opportunity to give him the opportunity to return to Blue », justified the French coach when questioned about his choice to select Gusto. A missed opportunity. Taken behind his back, overwhelmed in duels and unable to secure his side, the 23-year-old was undoubtedly the worst French player on the pitch.

Faulty on the first Italian goal signed Pio Esposito after being overwhelmed on his side (26th) by Cambiaghi, Malo Gusto then caused a penalty, certainly missed by Sebastiano Esposito. Two situations which weighed heavily on his performance, while the full-back was greatly expected. Unable to reassure defensively, he only won his first duel… in the 47th minute of play (2 out of 6). Enough to accentuate his completely upside-down match, in addition to his eight lost balls and his three fouls committed. In the 77th minute, his ordeal ended with the entry of last-minute call-up Saël Kumbedi, two minutes after the second Italian goal, coming from his side with this cross from Dimarco for Frattesi (63rd).

Destroyed on all sides, he was however spared by his coach who preferred to give a general assessment of the meeting: “ there were some good things, especially in the first half. Afterwards, we did less good things. Particularly in duels and in 1v1s, we were a little worse… The start of the second half was good too. It’s true that when we were in difficulty, we didn’t know how to react and stay within the framework set up. We did it in the first, and afterwards when they took the advantage, we dispersed a little, also on the defensive level. » While no player stands out for the moment on the right side, Malo Gusto seems to have burned his cards during this first match. Conversely, he is even going through a very difficult period in the selection, since France conceded eight goals in their last 166 minutes of play with him on the pitch. Not sure that he will be renewed on Sunday against Turkey.

Knicks rally to beat Cavaliers behind unlikely hero, expanded lineup to keep slim NBA Cup hopes alive

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mike Brown coached Wednesday's NBA Cup contest like a preseason game. He used a 12-man rotation, throwing out Tyler Nickel for his NBA debut and giving important minutes to sparsely used Pacôme Dadiet.

Brown, as he noted before the game, sees the scheduling benefits of being eliminated from Adam Silver's beloved tournament.

But it didn't matter. The Knicks (12-6), winners of six straight overall, surged in the fourth quarter behind unlikely hero Jose Alvarado. They avoided elimination — at least for a couple of days — with a 121-102 win over the collapsing Cavaliers, who blew an 11-point lead late in the third quarter.

Mikal Bridges celebrates after hitting a 3-pointer during the Knicks' 121-102 win over the Cavaliers on Nov. 25, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. Jason Szenes for NY Post

The Knicks (1-2 in group play) remain unlikely to advance. They'd need a victory over the Heat on Friday by at least seven points and a Sixers loss to the Pacers.

But the defending Cup champs aren't technically eliminated as of Thanksgiving. They were short-handed Wednesday without OG Anunoby (calf strain) and Miles McBride (left adductor tightness). They lost backup center Andre Drummond to a head injury in the first half.

Then they punished the Cavs (9-10) by winning the fourth quarter 39-17 with Alvarado scoring 16 of his 21 points in the final period. Mikal Bridges finished with an efficient 19 points. Karl-Anthony Towns extended his double-double streak to 15 (barely) with 12 points and 10 boards.

If and when Brown's squad is out of the tournament, the coach is looking at elimination as an NBA Cup half full.

Losing translates to an easier schedule for the Knicks, which wasn't lost on the coach before Wednesday's comeback win.

Brown knows because the NBA shared the possibilities. Advancement potentially brings another three road games — including the final in Indiana. And elimination means two games — including one at home — over a nine-day stretch.

Jose Alvarado celebrates after hitting a second 3-pointer in the fourth quarter of the Knicks' win over the Cavaliers. Jason Szenes for NY Post

"You look at it and you're like, 'Whoa, hell, that's a lot of days off if you don't advance,' " Brown said. "And so, again, you wanna compete, you wanna win, but if you don't … there's an opportunity if you got some guys banged up to get rest and or to work on some things in between the games that you have if you're not involved in the Cup."

Silver won't like that answer from the defending NBA Cup champs. But Brown was speaking from experience. The Knicks added a long road trip to their schedule last season because of the NBA Cup, then added an 83rd game with the final.

The results were phenomenal for the Knicks. They won the Cup, earned the prize money (each player won over $500,000) and months later captured the NBA championship.

Josh Hart reacts during the second quarter of the Knicks' win over the Cavaliers at Madison Square Garden. Jason Szenes for NY Post

But the Knicks, as an organization, didn't fully embrace Silver's tournament. It took them nearly a full year to hang the banner.

Then Wednesday, the coach of the Cup champs was openly espousing the virtues of being eliminated.

No crying over a spilled NBA Cup. For now, though, the Cup is still on the table.

Mikal Bridges rises up for a layup during the second quarter of the Knicks' win over the Cavaliers at Madison Square Garden. Jason Szenes for NY Post

"When I talk about the Cup, it adds a level of stress maybe on your mind and your body because the games are intense as you go along and you're playing more games in a short amount of time," Brown said. "I remember last year it was like, 'Game, Game, Game.' And then you win the Cup and you celebrate it that night and you hop on a plane and go play in Indiana the next night. And so it's great. You like the competition and the setup but it adds a lot of stress to you physically and mentally. I'm saying this because especially with the extended season we had last year and how taxing the games were for our playoff run mentally and physically, I'm trying to be smart."

LaMelo Ball injury update: Timberwolves star hurt late in win vs. Thunder

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Minnesota Timberwolves were already without their biggest star due to injury. Now they may have lost their second-best player, at least for the short term.

Late in Minnesota's game Wednesday, Nov. 25 against the Oklahoma City Thunder, with 2:29 left in the fourth quarter, point guard LaMelo Ball was defending a play in the middle of the paint, when he jumped and landed awkwardly, rolling his left ankle. Ball immediately grabbed at his foot and writhed on the floor in obvious discomfort. He remained on the floor for several moments before being helped toward the bench.

Trainers assessed Ball's foot while he sat on the end of the bench. After some minutes, he eventually got up and limped toward the locker room.

Ball scored 19 points on 7-of-18 shooting before leaving the game, adding 8 assists and 7 rebounds.

Ball is an absence the Timberwolves cannot afford. The team has been without All-Star Anthony Edwards, the most dynamic shooting guard in the NBA, since the fifth game of the regular season. Edwards underwent an arthroscopic procedure on his knee to clean up the area and reduce swelling.

Despite Edwards' absence, the Timberwolves have thrived, sprinting out to a 14-5 record, good for third place in the Western Conference.

Guard Ayo Dosunmu and forward Jaden McDaniels have also had a big offensive impact with Edwards sidelined, and if Ball is forced to miss some time, even more will be asked of the pair.

The Timberwolves would eventually beat the Thunder in their game, 113-106.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: LaMelo Ball injury update: Timberwolves star hurt late in win vs. Thunder

Rams move ahead 20-13 on Blake Corum TD

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Rams let Blake Corum do most of the heavy lifting on their opening drive of the second half.

Corum ran nine time for 35 yards during a 15-play Rams drive and the final yard he picked up was a touchdown that broke a tie and put the Rams up 13-10. The drive opened the third quarter and ate up eight minutes of playing time.

The Rams faced three third downs on the drive and had Matthew Stafford throw to wide receiver Konata Mumpfield each time. The first two were completions that picked up 30 yards and the third was initially ruled a touchdown on the field. Replays showed Mumpfield did not have control of the ball in the end zone, but the Rams still got a first down due to a defensive holding penalty on the day.

Corum has 16 carries for 58 yards and Mumpfield has four catches for 69 yards.

Google Adds Pageburst & 11 Other Existing Bots To Its Crawler Docs via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

Google's crawler docs now list pageburst, which renders pages for Google Ads and ignores robots.txt, plus more existing user agents to check in server logs.

Google identified pageburst as a user agent that renders pages to measure Google Ads quality and landing page safety and ignores robots.txt rules. Its two strings look like a browser’s and do not contain “Googlebot”; a bot rule created using “Googlebot” will not capture them.

Pageburst is one of 12 existing user agents Google added to its crawler documentation on Nov. 23, along with a corrected entry for APIs-Google. Google’s crawling documentation changelog explains:

“These crawlers and fetchers are not new; we’re documenting existing high-volume clients that are more likely to show up in server logs and that we’ve received questions about.”

The 12 belong to Google products other than Search, such as Google Ads, Gmail and Google Cloud.

Google’s special-case crawlers page, which lists crawlers used by specific Google products, says pageburst “renders web pages for Google Ads quality, landing-page safety, and page understanding.” The entry adds that “it’s unaffected by crawling preferences.”

The desktop string identifies as Chrome on Linux and the mobile one as Chrome for iOS on an iPad, and both include “compatible; pageburst.”

AdsBot, which checks web page ad quality for Google Ads, has its own robots.txt token. Pageburst’s entry lists no token and says the user agent “ignores robots.txt rules.”

The highlights show what to look for in server logs and where Google states its robots.txt policy.

Google’s documentation says user-agent strings can be faked. Special-case crawlers use the IP addresses defined in Google’s special-crawlers.json; these have a reverse-DNS match of rate-limited-proxy-***-***-***-***.google.com. The pageburst entry does not say what occurs when a website blocks the Google Ads check.

Besides pageburst, each of the five other newly documented special-case entries says the user agent “ignores robots.txt rules.”

Google’s user-triggered fetchers page covers the six newly documented fetchers and says that because a user requested the fetch, these fetchers “generally ignore robots.txt rules.” Three of those (AppEngine-Google, Google-Apps-Script and Google-Cloud-Tasks) send requests for code running within Google’s platform-based products. The remaining three are GoogleImageProxy, which is used to load image content into email applications such as Gmail, along with Google Web Preview and the PageSpeed Insights fetcher, both with strings ending in Chrome-Lighthouse.

Several of the newly listed user agents seem to use standard browser strings. For example, a string from GoogleImageProxy appears similar to Firefox 11 on Windows NT 5.1; the strings for Google Web Preview and PageSpeed Insights, and one of PlayStore-Google’s, were created using Chrome strings. A filter that only identifies “Googlebot” would miss all of these.

Google has moved APIs-Google, which makes requests to the webhook notification systems developers create within Google Cloud and Google Workspace APIs, from the list of special-case crawlers to the list of user-triggered fetchers. APIs-Google’s push requests, the changelog says, “come from the user-triggered fetcher IP ranges and ignore robots.txt rules.” Google added a missing semicolon after “APIs-Google” in its documented string and tells developers to modify any code that was previously using a hardcoded version of the earlier string.

Prior to the Nov. 23 update, Google had included robots.txt as a method to block requests from APIs-Google in its documentation. In an Oct. 21 capture, the robots.txt token was present on the special-case page. On Oct. 30, a capture of the APIs-Google page showed “Use robots.txt” as one of two methods to block requests from APIs-Google. Now the APIs-Google page says that it “ignores robots.txt rules”; for controlling the delivery of notifications, the page points to either the developer who created the notification system or Google Cloud’s abuse form for unexpected requests.

Pageburst renders pages for ads in Google and does not follow robots.txt. Check for “pageburst” in the log entries for your servers and CDNs, verify with Google’s procedures that the IP addresses are in its special-crawler ranges, and see if your pages were delivered to the verified requests, or if a challenge or a 403 was returned.

Roger Montti covered Google’s advice on Nov. 18 that general bot blocking may keep Googlebot from crawling content when moving a CDN; the same advice should be applied to other Google clients that do not have the name Googlebot.

Google’s special-case and user-triggered pages both say their lists aren’t exhaustive and cover the requestors “more likely to show up in log files.” The crawling documentation changelog, which is where Google logs additions like these, has an RSS feed.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

Watch Kevin Durant become fifth player in NBA history to score 33,000 points.

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kevin Durant is as pure a scorer as the game has ever seen.

Wednesday night he made history as only the fifth player in NBA history to score 33,000 points.

As noted, the only other players to reach this number are all legends: LeBron James, Kareem Abdul-Jabbar, Karl Malone and Kobe Bryant.

Durant will end the night just a little more than 600 points behind Kobe for fourth all time on the NBA scoring list, and Durant should pass him later this season.

Crypto host who once promoted a $50 billion memecoin vision now tells traders to get out

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ansem’s wallet hack exposed security risks at Bullpen and prompted renewed debate over crypto's reputation.

Jonah, co-host of the 1000x podcast, wrote on Nov. 24 to “get out while you still can” after crypto trader Ansem said his wallet had been hacked.

Jonah predicted that staying in crypto would carry a stigma. At 03:07 UTC on Nov. 25, trading app Bullpen said the known impact of its breach was limited to 19 early beta accounts.

At 03:53 UTC, Ansem advised users to withdraw their funds until he could confidently say they were safe.

Ansem’s initial post said he suspected that someone had accessed the Bullpen profile where he had imported the private key for a Solana wallet he used on Pump.fun, and Jonah’s quote-post came in response to that claim.

Bullpen’s first public notice came later Tuesday and identified Ansem as one of the users affected.

In its initial notice, Bullpen gave a preliminary range of 18 to 19 affected accounts, all opened between April and June 2024. The company said it took the affected test service offline.

Its Wednesday update gave a figure of 19 accounts and said it had seen no further movement of the funds at that point.

Bullpen said the attacker took over the older accounts, exposing wallets users had imported into them. For users who had imported a wallet, the access Bullpen described extended beyond the trading app to funds in that wallet.

The private key Ansem said he imported could authorize transfers from the wallet he used on Pump.fun. The company said 99.8% of the funds taken came from one account, which it did not name.

Bullpen reported 19 affected accounts from its early beta.

Bullpen said taking over an affected trading account could expose a wallet its user had already imported.

Bullpen’s count covers affected accounts. Its notices leave the total losses and initial method of account access unresolved.

Diagram based on Bullpen’s Nov. 24 account-takeover explanation and its Nov. 25 count of affected accounts. The connector shows an access relationship described by Bullpen.

Bullpen’s count covers affected accounts. Its notices leave the total losses and initial method of account access unresolved.

Bullpen added that it was working with the FBI and other authorities, along with the services involved, to freeze and recover the funds. Ansem said he tried to freeze the money while it was still in USDC, but the attempt required a court order.

The updates describe efforts to recover the money and leave the recovered amount undisclosed.

In his follow-up, Ansem said two of his Solana wallets were compromised, identifying them as the wallet he imported from Pump.fun and a wallet already on Bullpen.

He attributed the breach to a weakness in older accounts that he said let someone bypass Bullpen’s two-factor login. Ansem also said a separate BitGo wallet requiring multiple signatures, which he said held 49% of the ANSEM token supply, was safe and unmoved.

An ANSEM sell-off unfolded on Nov. 24, driven by a large ANSEM sell-off from a publicly labeled wallet. Bitquery reported about $2.57 million in USDC at one Arbitrum address at 17:00 UTC that day.

Jonah’s comments drew a quote-post from user soby and a “This you?” reply from @CupOJoseph. In another reply, Jonah said he liked Ansem and had helped launch a token a couple of years earlier.

Jonah had published a BODEN roadmap in April 2024. The post imagined the memecoin listing on the New York Stock Exchange at a $50 billion valuation and becoming a reserve currency.

In a separate post early Wednesday, he distinguished between launching a token he considered worthless and selling it, which he said made someone a scammer.

In his own post Tuesday, 1000x co-host Avi Felman wrote that “crypto is fine,” arguing the industry grew stronger through previous downturns.

Player Grades: Cavs at Knicks – Another fourth quarter implosion

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Cleveland Cavaliers took another painful loss to the New York Knicks after going off the rails in the fourth quarter.

Grades are based on our usual expectations for each player.

30 points, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 steals, 2 blocks, 2 turnovers

Mitchell was lights out in the first half, storming downhill and busting New York's point-of-attack defense. The Knicks had no answer for him as Mitchell began the game 9-for-10 from inside of two-point range.

His defensive activity was strong, as well, with Mitchell racking up stocks throughout the game.

The fourth quarter was less efficient. Mitchell's drives became erratic, including one play where he was lost in the trees twice in the same possession. There's value in putting pressure on the rim, but Mitchell has to do a better job of moving away from pressure when the time calls for it.

It was hard to choose between Mitchell and Harden for Player of the Game. Mitchell's defense tipped the scales.

28 points, 5 assists, 1 rebound, 4 turnovers

Good things happen when Harden touches the paint. He's looked more spry and youthful in recent games, dancing with his dribble to work into the restricted area and create from there. Whether it was shooting a floater, kicking to the perimeter, or dropping it into Jarrett Allen's lap — Harden put on a clinic on how to drive the ball.

Unfortunately, Harden shot 1-of-8 from downtown and wasn't able to sprint through the finish line as everything went wrong in the fourth quarter.

10 points, 5 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals

Allen was able to roam freely on defense while Harden accepted the challenge of guarding Karl-Anthony Towns on the perimeter. This strategy worked throughout the night as Cleveland was able to safely navigate New York's off-ball actions with Allen standing in the paint to clean up any mistakes. It wasn't until the wheels came off in the fourth quarter, when the Knicks were able to sprint in transition and break the mundanity of the half-court, that Allen became nonconsequential.

8 points, 5 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 steals, 2 blocks

It feels like I'm trying to figure out what to expect from Watson. He hasn't been featured enough in the offense for me to know where he fits. Watson only attempted four shots tonight and didn't spend much time with the ball in his hands.

Proctor had a rough shooting night (1-for-6) but was able to bolster the Cavaliers' defense by being aggressive away from the ball. Proctor does a good job of using his body and bumping guys who try to cut around him. The Cavs could use more of that mentality.

Sometimes, Thomas plays basketball like an adrenaline junkie. He bursts around the court and tries to get involved in every defensive play. That's helped him cause a handful of turnovers, like when he jump-scared Josh Hart, forcing him to lose the ball out of bounds in the second half.

This was not a standout game for Hezonja. He only attempted one shot in 10 minutes and finished as a minus-14.

Merrill hasn't had it going this season. He shot 1-of-7 from downtown tonight and is shooting 32% from deep this season.

6 points, 6 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 turnovers

Tyson is in a funk. It's that simple. He hasn't looked as confident or spry as he was last season. The Cavs will have to figure out how to correct this if they want to reach their ceiling this season. Tyson is too important as a potential sixth man for this to continue.

His last shot attempt was swatted into the stands.

Bryant gave the Cavs good minutes tonight by boxing out and cleaning the glass. I'm glad we were able to end this on a positive note.

U2 Salutes Dolly Parton With Recreation of Their ‘Torn’ Duet in NFL Thanksgiving Eve Halftime Show

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Senior Music Writer and Chief Music Critic

U2 paid tribute to Dolly Parton as part of its halftime performance Wednesday night at the NFL‘s first-ever Thanksgiving Eve game. The “duet” between a live Bono and a recording of Parton from last summer was the centerpiece of a highly promoted 10-minute set between halves of the contest between the Los Angeles Rams and Green Bay Packers, streamed exclusively on Netflix.

U2 did not attempt to cram as many songs into their brief performance as other superstars have during halftime performances, getting in four. The band opened with an abridged “Sunday Bloody Sunday” and ended with “”Pride (in the Name of Love)” and Where the Streets Have No Name” — only squeezing in one song from their new album, the Parton duet, “Torn,” with images of Dolly projected onto three huge curtains that dropped around their stage at the 50-yard line.

Dolly Forever <3 U2 pays tribute to Dolly Parton with “Torn” during their halftime performance pic.twitter.com/c4CfeoQiXi

About a hundred extras took part in the performances — forming not a marching band, but a marching choir. U2 was also joined by the Womack Sisters, a vocal trio that has just been nominated for best new artist at the Grammys.

The dancers first appeared as part of “Sunday Bloody Sunday,” which included an interpolation of the Supremes’ “Stop in the Name of Love,” rendered as something more somber, as performed by the Womack Sisters, to better fit in with U2’s theme of civil war. Bono repeated the “torn” part of that early ’80s anthem as a segue into the song “Torn,” as he and the spectral Parton harmonized on the recent song about a family at war. After one last look at Dolly, the curtains dropped to the field and the dancers formed three concentric circles around the band’s platform, repeating the familiar strains of of “Where the Streets Have No Name” and then “Pride (in the Name of Love)” as the Womacks joined U2 on stage.

U2 performs 'Where The Streets Have No Name' at the NFL Thanksgiving Eve halftime show. pic.twitter.com/dXhj4KQbPk

Technically, five U2 songs made their way into the set, as eagle-eyed or -eared U2 fans were able to catch a bit of “American Obituary” during “Sunday Bloody Sunday.” The song, which appeared on a recent EP from the band, references the killing by ICE of Renee Good. Related Stories Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Ceremony Gets Fired Up With Live-Wire Numbers From U2, the Chili Peppers, Usher, Cynthia Erivo, Sienna Spiro, Aerosmith and More Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Designates the Troubadour as a Landmark Venue; Going Forward, L.A. Will Host Hall's Induction Ceremony 'At Least Every Other Year' (EXCLUSIVE)

The medley was so not overly jam-packed, compared to some Super Bowl halftimes, that U2 used part of its allotted 10 minutes walking to the stage at the beginning and walking back out through the assembled marchers at the end.

Although this was the NFL’s first such Thanksgiving Eve halftime show, it is in a very recent tradition of superstars doing holiday halftimes; the previous ones have just been for Christmas.

U2 and their team reportedly approached the NFL themselves with the idea of doing a halftime show — obviously timed to capitalize not just on families gathered around the TV before a holiday, but, from U2’s perspective, following closely on the heels of the Nov. 13 release of “Carnaval de Luz,” the band’s first album of new material in nine years. The album debuted at No. 4 on the latest Billboard 200 chart.

It was U2’s third tango with the NFL, albeit its first in two decades. The Irish band famously did the Super Bowl halftime show in 2002, with an emphasis on honoring the victims of 9/11. In 2006, the group performed in celebration of the opening of the Louisiana Superdome after Hurricane Katrina.

The group has kept up a heavy cycle of broadcast and streaming promotions before and since the new album release, including a surprise opening to the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame ceremony at L.A.’s Peacock Theatre on Nov. 14, which is being edited into a special to be broadcast Dec. 8 on ABC.

Fosun International Named "Sustainability Leading Enterprise" by Bloomberg Businessweek/Chinese Edition

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

HONG KONG, Nov. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- On 25 November, the 8th "ESG Leading Enterprises 2026" award ceremony, co-organized by Bloomberg Businessweek/Chinese Edition and Deloitte, a globally renowned accounting firm, was successfully held in Hong Kong. The event was honored to feature a keynote speech and award presentation by Mr. Joseph H.L. Chan, JP, Under Secretary for Financial Services and the Treasury of the Government of Hong Kong Special Administrative Region. Fosun International was honored as a "Sustainability Leading Enterprise", in recognition of its outstanding achievements and strong market position across ESG strategy, corporate governance, social responsibility, environmental protection, information disclosure, and stakeholder engagement.

This year, 9 companies were honored as "Sustainability Leading Enterprises" in Category I - market capitalization over HKD12 billion, all of which are major Hong Kong listed companies, including Henderson Land Development, Sun Hung Kai Properties, HUTCHMED, The Hong Kong and China Gas, China Overseas Land & Investment, China Unicom, etc.

Operating Responsibly Worldwide, Earning Sustained ESG Recognition

Rooted in China, Fosun has been developing globally while remaining committed to its two key growth drivers of "globalization" and "innovation". Fosun is one of the few Chinese companies that is equipped with global operational and investment capabilities, and has built strong capabilities in technology and innovation. Drawing on its well-established global business network, Fosun operates responsibly in more than 40 countries and regions, helping improve lives and create sustainable value worldwide.

In recent years, Fosun has demonstrated outstanding performance in various aspects of ESG. To date, Fosun International's MSCI ESG Rating has been upgraded to the highest rating of AAA. It has been included in the S&P Global Sustainability Yearbook 2026 and also named among the Top 1% in the S&P Global Sustainability Yearbook (China Edition) 2026 for the third consecutive year. Its Hang Seng Sustainability Rating has been upgraded from AA- to AA+. It has also been selected as a constituent of the Hang Seng Corporate Sustainability Benchmark Index for the seventh consecutive year and included as a constituent of the Hang Seng ESG 50 Index. Additionally, Fosun International's FTSE Russell ESG score has consistently been higher than the global industry average and it has remained a constituent of the FTSE4Good Index Series for five consecutive years.

Leveraging Innovation to Deliver Lasting Social Impact

Fosun adheres to an innovation-driven strategy, continuously delivering innovative achievements across its businesses to better serve society. As of 30 June 2026, Fosun Pharma had cumulatively supplied more than 460 million vials of artesunate for injection worldwide, saving more than 92 million patients with severe malaria. Its independently developed HANSIZHUANG (serplulimab) is the world's first anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody approved for the first-line treatment of small cell lung cancer, and the first anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody approved for perioperative treatment of gastric cancer. It is now available in more than 50 countries and regions. HLX43, a core asset in the innovative pipeline, is a potential best-in-class (BIC) broad-spectrum anti-tumor PD-L1 antibody-drug conjugate (ADC). It has demonstrated preliminary clinical efficacy characterized by high efficacy and low toxicity across multiple solid tumors, including non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC), with over 1,500 patients enrolled globally.

Fosun remains committed to global cooperation and shared success, striving to make medical technologies more accessible and affordable to more patients. Yi Kai Da (ejilunsai injection), China's first approved CAR-T cell therapy, is now available across a network of over 190 treatment centers spanning 29 provinces, autonomous regions, and municipalities nationwide. More than 1,500 patients with relapsed or refractory lymphoma have received the therapy, with the initial clinical trial patient remaining cancer-free for more than eight years. This year, the da Vinci SP surgical system was included in the catalog under the "Hong Kong and Macau Medicine and Equipment Connect" scheme. It was also admitted into the Special Review Procedure for Innovative Medical Devices by the National Medical Products Administration (NMPA). The Ion Robotic Bronchoscopy (Ion System) received marketing approval and was launched in the Chinese mainland, extending the use of minimally invasive robotic technology beyond surgery to the diagnosis and treatment of lung diseases. Exablate Neuro is a Magnetic Resonance-guided Focused Ultrasound (MRgFUS) system that has treated over 30,000 patients across more than 210 medical centers worldwide, offering a new treatment option for patients with medication-refractory essential tremor or tremor-dominant Parkinson's disease.

Actively Responding to Climate Change and Promoting Low-Carbon Transition

To address climate change, Fosun actively supports China's "dual carbon" goals of carbon peaking and carbon neutrality, advancing initiatives in carbon neutrality, energy conservation, emissions reduction, and climate risk management. In 2026, the Company continued its voluntary disclosure practice by releasing its fourth Climate Information Disclosures Report, further enhancing the systematic approach and transparency of its climateârelated disclosures. The Bund Finance Center (BFC), the Group's main office and a landmark in Shanghai, introduced and followed the LEED Certification, which is hailed as the "Oscar Award" in the green building industry, from the early planning stage. BFC also obtained the LEED Platinum certification in 2022 with the highest score globally.

Preserving and Sharing Intangible Cultural Heritage, Promoting Sustainable Oriental Lifestyle Aesthetics

Over the years, Fosun has taken a comprehensive approach to preserving intangible cultural heritage, integrating it into modern daily life while weaving sustainability into oriental lifestyle aesthetics. Fosun's Grand Yuyuan is working to create an urban cultural parlor that showcases intangible cultural heritage. Take the Yuyuan Lantern Festival, a national intangible cultural heritage event, as an example, in 2026, the Year of the Horse, the festival featured 6 themed lantern zones for the first time and drew a total of 6 million visitors. Supported by Fosun's global platform, the Yuyuan Lantern Festival continues to put its commitment to carbon neutrality into practice, leveraging low-carbon technologies to create environmentally friendly lantern displays. It has also expanded internationally, with editions held in Paris, France, Hanoi, Vietnam, and Bangkok, Thailand. The festival has become a vital platform for Sino-foreign cultural exchange, continuously telling "China Stories" to the world and conveying the vision of sustainable living.

Committed to Business for Good, Actively Giving Back to Society

In order to better promote the fulfillment and implementation of corporate social responsibility, Fosun Foundation was established in 2012. It has been making unremitting efforts in areas such as global emergency relief, rural revitalization, health, education, culture and art, youth development, to create social value. Of which, since its launch at the end of 2017, Fosun Foundation's Rural Doctors Program has covered 78 counties in 16 provinces, cities, and autonomous regions, supporting 25,000 rural doctors and benefiting 3 million rural families.

Looking ahead, Fosun will continue to pursue innovation and operate responsibly worldwide, striving to build a more responsible, inclusive, and sustainable future.

Pochettino fumes at ‘impossible’ decisions after Man Utd draw with Sporting

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino was left frustrated by several refereeing decisions during Manchester United’s 2-2 draw with Sporting Lisbon, insisting his side were punished in key moments of the game.

Manchester United had to settle for a point in Portugal after a chaotic evening away from home. Bruno Fernandes scored twice, but Luis Suarez also found the net on two occasions to deny Pochettino his first win as United head coach.

Referee Szymon Marciniak came under scrutiny for a series of controversial decisions. He ruled out an Amad Diallo goal after penalising Youri Tielemans for a foul in the build-up, blowing his whistle before the ball crossed the line. That meant VAR could not intervene on the goal and it was not given.

The Polish official also awarded Sporting a first-half penalty and disallowed what would have been a late United winner. He blew whistle on Patrick Dorgu for pushing goalkeeper Kaique Pereira, disallowing the goal.

Pochettino did not hide his frustration when asked about the decisions after the match.

“I think your first question is difficult to answer because I think everyone knows all that was, it was impossible,” he said after the game.

“You know, it’s difficult to understand why that happened on the field. It’s difficult to find a game where you get punished [so much] in one game. We were punished a lot in key actions.”

United also conceded an 80th-minute equaliser after Senne Lammens failed to hold a cross, allowing Suarez to score from close range.

However, Pochettino was keen to defend his goalkeeper rather than blame him for the mistake.

“I think, please, it’s to blame us,” the United boss said.

He acknowledged that his side needed to manage such situations better.

“But of course, we need to accept, we accept the challenge and we are here to try to improve, you know, the small details that for sure we will improve.”

Pochettino’s frustration was understandable after two similar incidents of bad refereeing in his first two games at United. Leny Yoro’s red card against Liverpool was later overturned on appeal, while a series of errors against Sporting cost him yet another victory. But despite the errors, Pochettino also recognises that his players need to improve their decision-making during games.

Nepal: Predatory Lending Abuses Exacerbated by Conflict in the Gulf

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Click to expand Image Loan shark victims demand justice after 38 days of protest in Kathmandu, Nepal, March 7, 2024. © 2024 Skanda Gautam/SOPA Images/Sipa via AP Photo Thousands of borrowers face exorbitant interest on fraudulent loan agreements taken out to fund labor migration and other needs.The Nepali government has criminalized abusive practices, but police and courts often side with loan sharks against distressed borrowers.Lost job opportunities due to war in the Gulf region have deepened the crisis facing many borrowers.

(Tokyo) – Authorities in Nepal should hold illegal moneylenders accountable for abuses against borrowers, including unlawful land dispossession, arbitrary eviction, threats and acts of violence, and sexual exploitation, Human Rights Watch said today. Conflicts in the Gulf region, where many Nepalis are migrant workers facing exorbitant recruitment fees, have made more people vulnerable to extortion by illegal lenders.

“Despite government commitments to protect borrowers, illegal practices are rife in Nepal’s informal credit sector, leaving those with the fewest resources dependent on moneylenders who can take everything they own,” said Meenakshi Ganguly, Asia deputy director at Human Rights Watch. “The government of Prime Minister Balendra Shah should carry out the government’s commitments to ensure that the law against fraudulent and abusive lending is consistently enforced.”

Human Rights Watch interviewed 18 borrowers, as well as local government officials, lawyers, and activists from six districts in the Terai region where informal money lending is rampant, reviewed government reports, and examined over 50 cases in Nawalparasi district.

Human Rights Watch found that many families are struggling with unmanageable debt particularly as migrant workers in the Gulf States during 2026 have faced reduced incomes or involuntary travel delays ahead of starting jobs due to the conflict between the US, Israel, and Iran. War-induced disruption amplified pre-existing abuses, including reliance on unauthorized individual agents and unreasonable recruitment fees. Human Rights Watch previously found that companies in Gulf states play a critical role in driving these illegal costs by refusing to cover costs and fees to recruiters that they know will be borne by workers.

Government investigations have shown that, under Nepal’s informal credit practices, a borrower signs or thumbprints an unsecured personal loan contract, known locally as kapali tamasuk, which can falsely list a principal amount three to ten times more than the cash received. These loans, known as meter byaj, in some cases compound at annual rates ranging from 36 percent to 60 percent or more, compared with less than 10 percent charged by banks.

As low-income households that had relied on remittances disrupted by the war have shifted their limited resources toward expenses for food, fuel, and healthcare, they have been increasingly unable to repay their loans, leading to a surge in defaults. In response, some moneylenders are forcing evictions or land transfers, including through violence, in the worst cases tying people into generational debt. Nepal authorities are not pushing for effective long term solutions beyond encouraging and facilitating debtors and creditors to enter pre-court settlements.

Following the outbreak of conflict in the Persian Gulf, in March 2026, the Nepal government suspended both institutional and individual labor permits to protect its citizens, including to Saudi Arabia, the United Arab Emirates, Qatar, and Kuwait, with an estimated 2,000 Nepalis denied labor permits daily. Thousands were stopped from travelling at the international airport despite possessing valid medical clearances, verified visas, and binding employment contracts, while administrative delays also led foreign firms to cancel labor contracts. However, the authorities failed to step in to protect aspiring migrant laborers who still had to service their high interest meter byaj loans, leaving them exposed to violent enforcement by loan sharks.

“They did not just hit my father to get the money,” said one victim. “They beat him in the middle of the road during the day so that everyone else in the village would see what happens when you cannot pay the meter byaj. The local goons told us, ‘If you don’t sign over the land title today, your family will receive a corpse tomorrow.’”

While abuses, including violent attacks and forced evictions, appear to be increasing due to conflict related disruptions, many moneylenders have long wielded political and economic power over borrowers. Claiming that the justice system routinely favors the moneylenders, borrowers have staged three extended protests in Kathmandu over the last four years—the longest for 442 days—and three long marches. Each time, the government agreed to resolve debt issues but failed to honor its commitments to protect victims. Consequently, the borrowers have regularly protested at district and provincial levels as well.

Although government has criminalized such loans, borrowers and activists in Madhesh Province said that police have repeatedly refused to file reports when victims seek protection. Instead, they said, the police typically dismiss abuses and intimidation by moneylenders as “private civil disputes.”

“When the moneylender came to break our door, we ran to the police, but the officers told us to go home and settle personal transactional disputes ourselves,” said a Rautahat district migrant worker.

The police also back moneylenders when they use signed blank checks and banking laws to threaten defaulters with arrest, or use informal deeds as the basis to seize land and evict families. “The lender made me sign a blank check when he gave me a loan, and later on he filled it for 10 lakhs (US$6,500) and sent the police to threaten me with jail when the check bounced,” said a Siraha district farmer.

Borrowers also cite cases in which the authorities do not intervene even when lenders use patently unlawful loan agreements to obtain asset foreclosure orders. “The lender told me openly that he owns the courts and the administration,” said Shiv Shanker from Gaur, Rautahat district, who after facing abuses from a moneylender, is leading protests. “When the state shuts its doors on a poor farmer, death feels like the only place left to hide from this meter byaj.”

On May 5, 2023, the government criminalized informal loans as an “improper transaction,” and declared that forging loan contracts, threatening borrowers, or fabricating loan amounts, are punishable by up to seven years in prison. However, enforcement remains weak. Successive governments have established inquiry commissions to address the crisis, but over 65,000 complaints registered with inquiry commissions remain unresolved due to a lack of legal provisions and administrative bottlenecks.

These failures have contributed to deadly outcomes including suicide. Santidevi Mahato from Biratnagar died by suicide on June 21, following threats from a moneylender because she could not service her debt, people interviewed said. After borrowing 150,000 Nepali rupees (US$975) in October 2015, she had repaid 400,000 Nepali rupees (US$2,600) in installments. Yet, violating their agreement, the moneylender seized her land and threatened to evict her from her house.

Under international human rights law and standards, governments have the obligation to take steps to protect people from serious rights abuses caused by private actors, such as moneylenders, including through investigating and prosecuting serious crimes.

On July 17, the government led by Prime Minister Balendra Shah concluded a nine-point pact with protest leaders to eliminate predatory lending, after debt victims began a march to Kathmandu. The government should take immediate legal and policy steps to institutionalize its commitment, Human Rights Watch said. Nepal’s donors and international partners should assist the government to establish systematic debt relief, and grievance mechanisms that can support debt restructuring.

“The Balendra Shah government was elected with the promise of change, to overturn existing oppressive structures,” Ganguly said. “More closely regulating moneylenders and ending their chokehold on farmers and laborers is a crucial step in ensuring lasting social justice.”

Loan Sharks and Migrant Workers from Nepal

Nepal’s informal credit system is rooted in its feudal history, where local elites used predatory debt to trap peasants in tenancy and systems of generational servitude such as Kamaiya, Haliya, and Haruwa-Charuwa.

The present-day meter byaj system evolved around a spurt in labor migration during the decade-long civil war in Nepal, from 1996 to 2006, and rapidly spread after the 2007 political unrest in the Terai region. An estimated 7 million Nepalis work abroad, about half in India and a majority of the rest in Gulf countries, contributing a third of the national GDP. For many households, migration became one of the few viable ways to earn a living, while the absence of accessible and affordable institutional credit left them dependent on informal lenders to finance the costs of securing employment abroad.

Some local moneylenders and corrupt agents demanded exorbitant fees to help rural youth find jobs abroad, particularly in the Gulf States and Malaysia. Land or property was borrowers’ main collateral, but they often later discovered that their contracts were fraudulent, including falsified sums and forged documents. The schemes exploited bureaucratic complicity at government land offices to transfer property titles despite contracts being illegal, and borrowers lost their assets due to a lack of protection from the government.

In interviews, debt victims described devastating cycles in which migrant workers are forever bound to serving a predatory moneylender, while the authorities fail to intervene. Ramnath Musahar from Susta, Nawalparasi district, said the family borrowed to send two of his brothers to work in the Gulf States in 2015, but despite servicing the loan, their debt had grown enormously:

My brothers sent back Rs. 1,200,000 (US$8,000) from their wages, and we sold our plots [of land] to pay an extra Rs. 1,800,000 (US$11,000). But the lender went to the district court with a fake contract and claimed we still owe him Rs. 5,000,000 (US$33,000). The court looked only at the paper and our thumbprints; it did not look at our tears. The police came and threw our belongings into the dirt. We went from being independent farmers to beggars overnight.

Conflict Between the US, Israel, and Iran Exacerbates Debt Risks

Since the US and Israel began their assault on Iran on February 28, 2026, and Iran subsequently blocked and attacked ships traveling through the Strait of Hormuz and attacked sites across the Gulf States, the fallout is being felt far beyond the region.

In Nepal, UN agencies warned that “rising fuel prices, closure of shipping routes, and growing financial uncertainty are placing mounting pressure on economies, labor markets and vulnerable households.” According to the World Bank, Nepal’s real GDP growth could fall from 3.67 percent in 2024 to 2.3 percent in 2026 due to reduced tourism, higher transport costs, and supply chain snags. Rising inflation, a weakening currency leading to a rise in import costs, a spike in fuel costs, and stalled fertilizer supply have placed additional pressure on low-income households, and increased demands for overseas employment.

Hoping to seek jobs to support their families, many rural youths have taken loans from loan sharks, who have responded by raising rates. Many families were stuck servicing those loans, even as people were unable to travel because of conflict-related disruptions.

Workers already employed in the Gulf States are facing job losses, unpaid leave, wage reductions, and other disruptions that have reduced their ability to keep sending remittances. Compared with the same period the previous year, Gulf-bound labor approvals plummeted by 52 percent—from 228,165 down to 109,249—between the outbreak of the war in late February and mid-September, according to Department of Foreign Employment data. At least two Nepali workers have been killed due to the conflict, and 15 injured.

Many are returning to Nepal stripped of their anticipated income, and unable to service their loans. The authorities have failed to act as moneylenders crack down on defaulters, beating them, evicting them, and forcing land or asset transfers. The lenders are also seeking court-ordered asset foreclosures and even arrest warrants based on the loan contracts, including contracts that borrowers alleged contained fabricated loan amounts. As a Mahotari district borrower observed, “The law says meter byaj is a crime, but police demanded receipts I never got, so the thief walks free, and I am made the criminal.”

Women-led households, Dalits (considered an “untouchable” caste), and Indigenous ethnic communities have even fewer protections from violence such as beatings and sexual violence due to their caste, gender, and discrimination. One woman from Dhanusha district said she was raped by a moneylender under threat of eviction.

My husband was trapped in a labor camp abroad and couldn’t send money. The lender came to my house with the contract and said he would evict my children the next morning unless I “compromised” with him. He held our roof over my head like a weapon. I had to endure his abuse in silence because a poor woman in this village has no voice against a powerful man who holds her land paper.

Abuses by Moneylenders

While the conflict has exacerbated the crisis, debt-ridden families had already proliferated in Nepal, particularly in Terai districts. The authorities have failed to protect these families.

Chirai Chamar, a widowed Dalit worker in Paklihawa, Nawalparasi district, while struggling to gain control of her inherited family land, had borrowed 20,000 Nepali rupees (US$130). A moneylender offered to help with the legalities in exchange for a portion of that land. “A moneylender, from whom I had borrowed Rs. 20,000, came to me saying he will help me to acquire all the papers required to own my family land, and in return I need to give him two katthas (650 square meters) of land,” she said.

He then grabbed an additional four katthas (1336 square meters), paying Chamar a grossly undervalued sum of Rs. 150,000 (US$970). When rights activists intervened to reclaim Chamar’s land and restore her livelihood, local thugs threatened them and destroyed Chamar’s crops. Despite complaints, the police and the local officials failed to assist her.

Ashamaya (name changed), a resident of Thecho, Lalitpur, supported her family by running a small tea shop. However, when her father-in-law fell ill, she had to close the business to care for him. Left without income as the medical expenses mounted, she borrowed money from a moneylender, who charged an extortionate annual interest rate of 120 percent, which she could not pay. Under pressure, she took on another usurious loan simply to pay off the old one.

Although she settled that debt by selling a piece of land, she had to borrow again, this time to cover her husband’s medical costs, but she no longer had enough land for collateral. The moneylender coerced her into sex with multiple men after he forced her to sign a new contact for Rs. 2500,000 (UDS$16,167), amounting to five times her initial debt.

Seeking a Remedy

Debt victims have started to organize under various banners and groups, demanding that the government protect them from abusive loan sharks. In 2022, after hundreds walked barefoot for weeks from districts including Siraha, Dhanusha, Mahottari, Sarlahi, Kapilvastu, and Nawalparasi to stage protests in Kathmandu, the government at that time establisheda three-member Commission for the Investigation of Loan Sharking (CILS) in April 2023, to collect complaints, study structural issues, and resolve loan-sharking disputes.

By May 21, 2023, when the District Administration Offices stopped accepting these complaints, over 28,000 had been filed across 67 districts, with an alarming 21,552 cases concentrated in Madhesh Province. However, the commission only resolved and closed 5,115 loan disputes. Several victims reported abuses by district offices. “We went to the Chief District Officer expecting justice, but instead, the police and the lender ganged up on us, warned us that going to court would mean total land auction, and scared us into signing an agreement to pay half the inflated sum,” said a woman from Siraha.

As complaints persisted, including of violent encounters in which the police failed to intervene, the authorities largely resorted to symbolic agreements with the victims, followed by the repeated formation of new commissions. A commission was set up in March 2024, led by former high court judge Tej Bahadur Karki, and another in March 2025, led by Baburam Regmi. In November 2025, the Regmi Commission reported a total of 41,409 complaints registered in 43 districts. Of these, 6,393 cases had been resolved, while 35,016 complaints remain pending.

Each commission exposed the vast scale of the crisis but political commitments to break the stranglehold of moneylenders remain unfulfilled. Nawalparasi’s Deputy Chief District Officer Poshan Raj Bhandari told Human Rights Watch that, “The existing law will not be enough to resolve this problem; the government needs to think of a separate legal process to resolve this.”

When Prime Minister Balendra Shah’s government was elected in March 2026 with a large majority, victims had cause for hope. However, instead of the government ensuring a collective debt moratorium, victims say that moneylenders have convinced the police to issue a surge of arrest warrants against debtors.

In response, victims have organized new protests. Nigra Nabin, a leader of the protest movement, said that based on “past experiences” the victims “had three long-standing demands” including the nullification of all illegal agreements and the underlying loan contracts that support them; a new law to institutionalize these actions and establish special legal procedures; and a separate entity dedicated to ensuring justice for the victims.

On July 9, the anti-loan shark movement began an 11-day justice march from Janakpur to Kathmandu. On the sixth day of the march, Home Affairs Minister Sudhan Gurung traveled to Nijgadh to hold direct talks with the protesters. Following three days of negotiations, the government accepted the three core demands and signed an updated nine-point deal, leading the protesters to call off the march.

The victims are waiting. One of them, Sobita Devi Mahato, said: “If this is not resolved, the only resort I have is to go to the Home Ministry and hang myself there.”

RecommendationsHalt arbitrary arrests of borrowers.Prosecute exploitative lenders for crimes against defaulters including violent attacks, sexual exploitation, and threats.Impose penalties for predatory lending practices, including aggressive debt collection through forced eviction or land grabs.Stop district authorities and police from carrying out foreclosures and evictions based on illegal loan agreements.Draft a new law to end illegal loan agreements and usury-related abuses. Establish a legal hold on all land transfers and evictions tied to disputed informal loans.Provide assistance, including debt relief where possible, to migrant workers in crisis because of sudden loss of work due to circumstances beyond their control, including war.Devise and enforce laws and policies that are designed to protect borrowers, including interest rate caps, requirements that borrowers have full and clear information about loan terms, and proper assessment of borrowers’ ability to repay loans. Consult with victims and stakeholders while drafting new laws and policies to dismantle the abusive informal moneylending system.Expand access to affordable institutional credit for low-income households and strengthen oversight of labor recruitment so that workers are not forced to rely on abusive informal lenders to finance migration or other essential expenses.Expand access to decent work and livelihoods in Nepal and strengthen social security so that households are less likely to rely on debt-financed labor migration.Use bilateral labor instruments such as labor agreements with governments of destination countries to ensure that companies hiring Nepali migrant workers cover the costs and fees related to their recruitment as per the employer pays principle, conduct proper due diligence and effective monitoring of Nepali recruiters, and have reimbursement mechanisms in instances when workers have paid fees.

developed with travelers aged 75+, moove on reimagines luggage as discreet mobility aid

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Travel  |  Read Source →

combining luggage storage, walking support and an integrated resting perch, moove on by lorenzo rimini challenges the conventional aesthetics of assistive products.

Lorenzo Rimini edited by christina vergopoulou November 26, 2026 3 min

Designed by Lorenzo Rimini, Moove On reconfigures the conventional rolling suitcase into a multi-functional mobility aid for older travelers. Combining standard luggage storage with physical walking support and an integrated resting perch, the product moves away from the clinical aesthetic typically associated with assistive medical devices. Informed by field observations across European transit environments, user interviews, and co-design sessions with nine participants aged 75 and older, the design calibrates its proportions, internal compartment layout, load-bearing frame, and seating height around the physical ergonomics of later-life travel.

Developed by designer Lorenzo Rimini over an eight-month period, the project progressed through 45 iterative prototypes examining alternative form factors, handle geometries, structural reinforcements, and functional configurations. Moving from initial 3D printed scale studies to full-scale mockups, Rimini built a 1:1 Plexiglas prototype to verify spatial organization, balance, and visual proportions. The resulting object merges everyday travel utility with structural stability, supporting independent movement without visually signaling disability.

the rolling suitcase brings together mobility, storage, and a distinctive visual identitythe product replaces clinical assistive-device styling with contemporary luggage aesthetics

K-beauty stocks rally as Korean brands top Amazon bestseller lists globally ahead of Black Friday

📅 2026-11-26  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

South Korean beauty stocks jumped Thursday as the country's skincare brands dominated Amazon's bestseller rankings ahead of Black Friday.

Shares of K-beauty giant APR climbed 3.77%, while cosmetic contracts manufacturers Kolmar Korea and Cosmax gained 5.46% and 2.91%, respectively.

APR shares, in particular, have rallied sharply this week, up 28%, defying a broader market sell-off in Seoul markets, with the benchmark Kospi index down 2.65% during the same period.

A cosmetic sector index tracked by Meritz Securities had gained 12% this week until Wednesday, according to a Thursday note from the brokerage.

The rally reflects renewed optimism over earnings growth following strong performances by K-beauty brands across major e-commerce platforms in the U.S. and Europe with Black Friday sales already underway, as well as increasingly attractive valuations following months of share price declines, according to Meritz.

K-beauty products topped Amazon's skincare bestseller rankings across six major markets — the U.S., the U.K., Germany, France, Spain and Italy — as of Nov. 25, according to data compiled by Meritz Securities. Other Korean beauty brands, including Biodance, Illiyoon and Anua, also ranked among the top 10 in the U.S., the data showed.

As of Thursday morning, Korean beauty products occupied several of the top spots in Amazon's U.S. beauty and personal care category, the e-commerce website showed.

Meritz Securities expects APR shares to maintain their positive momentum through the year-end, with a more substantial rebound in early 2027.

The brokerage named APR and Cosmax as its top picks in the cosmetics sector. Korean beauty brands have established an "overwhelming presence" in the bestseller rankings of major e-commerce platforms during Black Friday sales, prompting the market to reassess the growth's potential, the brokerage said.

Bono Bowl Review: U2 Delivers Halftime Goods & Hits On Netflix & NFL’s First Thanksgiving Eve Game, With A Little Help From Dolly Parton

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Executive Editor, Legal, Labor & Politics

No matter how many hits and tricks U2 pulled out of the hat at L.A.’s SoFi Stadium, today’s Bono Bowl on Netflix was always going to live in the shadow of the streamer’s square dancing filled Beyoncé Bowl from Christmas Day 2024.

Accepting the inevitable, the elder statesmen of anthem rock took their halftime spin on stage at Netflix and the NFL’s first ever Thanksgiving Eve game to go full bread and circuses. Giving the people and the streamer what they wanted, the nine-minute show wasn’t as dramatic as the currently 13-13 match-up between the hometown L.A. Rams and the Green Bay Packers, but it didn’t have to be.

All U2 had to do tonight was show everyone they still have gas in the tank. Watch on Deadline

Under those criteria, in their first time headlining on Netflix, the Irish rockers succeeded.

Veering towards the tried and true, Bono, guitarist The Edge, bassiest Adam Clayton and drummer Larry Mullen Jr thundered through their Boomer classics of ‘Sunday Bloody Sunday,’ The Joshua Tree’s ‘Where The Streets Have No Name’ and MLK tribute ‘Pride (In The Name of Love)’. Add to that a sprinkle of The Supremes ‘Stop! In the Name of Love’ and some glorious vocals thrown in from singers and the beats of an on-field marching band and you have an All-American party – which is precisely what a NFL halftime show is supposed to be.

In a nod to their latest release and the times we live in, the second song in the set poignantly was the ‘Torn’ duet with Dolly Parton

Dolly Forever <3 U2 pays tribute to Dolly Parton with “Torn” during their halftime performance pic.twitter.com/c4CfeoQiXi

With the obvious exception of the inclusion of the late great Dolly, who was displayed on flowing curtains that came down from the stadium roof, it was a set that could have come almost anytime the last 40 years. In fact, besides ‘Torn’ (Parton’s last recording), every song in U2’s Thanksgiving Eve set from from when Ronald Reagan was President.

Still, it was a set that tried to turn the other cheek to the nostalgia of it all.

In many ways, Bono’s vow not to use backing tracks and the pre-halftime shot that Netflix showed of U2 heading through Sofi’s catacombs on gold carts sorta told you all you needed to know.

To their credit, unlike most Super Bowl halftime shows of the past dozen years, (and expected for Jay-Z’s Super Bowl LXI set on February 14 at Sofi), a relatively stripped down U2 didn’t bring put any super star guests Wednesday for the L.A. crowd. Walking out on the field to a chorus, the usually chatty band took the in-the-round stage with a “thank you for having us” and said little more.

Oddly ending a bit too quick to go to an ad, tonight’s shindig came halfway through the NFL’s first Thanksgiving Eve match with the Rams battling it out with Packers at the L.A. team’s always camera-ready stadium. A clear belt notch in the league’s desire to dramatically expand the incredibly lucrative football franchise, tonight’s much hyped game between the Packers and the Rams was given all the gloss by the Ted Sarandos and Greg Peter-run streamer of a playoff clash or the Super Bowl itself.

Having already played a few warm-up gigs, like opening the November 14 Rock and Roll Hall of Fame ceremony, during the extensive promotional tour this month for their recently released Carnaval De Luz album, tonight additionally gave U2 a chance to stretch their legs and chops for fans old and new. Aided by a stage that was rolled out and set up in less than 8-minutes, a 52-foot telescopic camera crane and a specialty camera coming down from the Sofi roof, U2’s Jesse Collins Entertainment produced set had a very 2026 professional shine.

For a big gesture band never shy to take the big stages in a big way, Netflix’s global reach can’t help U2 hawk a few more tickets for their upcoming Greatest Show on Earth tour with the best promo reel Netflix can buy. The tour begins in Mexico City in February. After shows in South America and Europe, the band and their unofficial 50th anniversary tour ends up in North America for a 15-stadium run starting in Miami on August 13.

At the same time, as the Rolling Stones, Bruce Springsteen and many other stadium-playing legacy acts will tell you, the role of old bands is to play the old hits – – no matter how good or bad the latest album is. Bono, the Edge, Mullen and Clayton knew that back in 2002 when they played Super Bowl XXXVI in the aftermath of the 9/11 terror attacks. Using their Elevation Tour set at NOLA’s Superdome, U2 in less than 20-minutes whipped off ‘Beautiful Day,’ ‘MLK’ and ‘Where The Streets Have No Name’ and paid tribute via curtain with the names of the fallen on it to those who lost their lives that horrible day in NYC.

One of the biggest bands in the world for 40 years, U2 haven’t toured since 2019, and they had a little bit to prove tonight, as they made clear.

Yet, the GameDay halftime show wasn’t their first time on stage this decade.

A 40-show stint at Vegas’ Sphere starting in September 2023 saw the band pulling it off Zoo TV style, even with an injured Mullen sitting it out. Supporting his mates, the now clearly all healed up drummer was in the Sin City crowd a couple of nights. Still, a big live draw since the mid-1980s no matter how their records charted, the Irving and Jeffrey Azoff’ managed U2 are leaving no opportunity unexplored this year in a hope to return to relevance.

Leaving no marketing stone unturned, even Sofi itself got in on the act Wednesday.

We are excited to welcome @U2 for their premiere appearance at SoFi Stadium during today's halftime show 🏈 And even more excited for their return on October 1 & 2! Tickets on sale on December 4 on Ticketmaster 🎟️ pic.twitter.com/KnB9CkgIQp

In that, with ads galore and L.A. beauty shots tonight, Netflix have finally worked out most of the kinks that marred some of their past live sports events. Fueled by play-by-play from Noah Eagle on play-by-play and analysis from Hall of Famer Luke Kuechly, the primetime result so far delivered a slick and seamless product that moved at the pace of the action on the field. Simply put, (and hear me Jeff Bezos), Netflix equaled what ESPN and Monday Night Football have been doing for ages – which is no small feat when you consider that the streamer’s initial live sporting event was just over three years ago with the mash-up golf tournament The Netflix Cup.

From the big picture perspective, tonight’s game and that halftime show can’t but help Netflix’s ongoing bid to FIFA for the 2030 World Cup. For U2, the ride may be a little vintage, but turns out there’s still quite a bit of gas in the old tank.

Cavs suffer familiar loss to Knicks; Mitchell’s 30 points not enough

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Cleveland Cavaliers have all but been eliminated from the NBA Cup after the New York Knicks, who outscored them 39-17 in the fourth quarter to pick up their second win over Cleveland this season.

The Cavs are now 2-2 in the cup, making it almost certain they will miss out on advancing to the next stage. There's always next year.

Cleveland's defensive focus and energy were much improved in the first half compared to their previous loss in Chicago. The Cavs played on a string, trusting each other by not overhelping — while seamlessly communicating through switches and containing New York's off-ball screening actions by working together.

Donovan Mitchell was relentlessly getting downhill throughout the first half. Mitchell opened the game shooting 8-for-9 from inside two-point range. He entered halftime with 20 points.

James Harden chipped in with 14 points on 8-for-8 shooting from the free-throw line in the first half. Mikal Bridges led the Knicks with 17 points on 8-of-11 shooting overall.

The Cavs hit the ground running in the third quarter, controlling the tempo and putting New York on the back foot before a myriad of fouls slowed the game to a crawl. This abrupt shift allowed the Knicks to storm back and make the game competitive again.

Cleveland found themselves trailing by double digits in the fourth quarter as the Knicks began turning defense into offense. A handful of errant turnovers from the Cavs' backcourt fueled the run.

Winning the possession battle has been an emphasis for the Cavs, especially in this matchup. The Knicks have historically punished Cleveland by grabbing offensive rebounds and making it impossible to end defensive possessions.

The Cavs started off on the right foot in that regard. They were outscoring New York 11-to-1 on second-chance points and had full control of the possession battle.

The Knicks grabbed every key rebound, forced backbreaking turnovers, and hit all of the big shots down the stretch to put this game out of reach. A fate the Cavs have suffered more than once to this team. As stated, it was a 22-point bludgeoning in the fourth quarter that closed the door.

Harden and Mitchell combined for 58 points. Both struggled in the fourth quarter after playing stellar basketball before. Their six shared turnovers felt like they came at the worst times.

The Cavs end the night officially below .500 with a 9-10 record. Not how anyone hoped this season would start.

NFL on Thanksgiving halftime shows: Ranking the 10 most memorable performances

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

NFL halftime shows have evolved since the beginning of the league. What began as marching bands and local high school bands has now turned into full productions.

Thanksgiving has featured big performances from top artists since 1997, when Reba McEntire performed at the first Salvation Army Red Kettle Kickoff halftime show, which kicks off the Salvation Army's annual Red Kettle Campaign and encourages fans to give back to those in need.

This year, three games will be played on Thanksgiving Day. Bears vs. Lions will be played at 1 p.m. ET on CBS and stream on Paramount+, Eagles vs. Cowboys will take place at 4:30 p.m. ET on FOX, and the Chiefs and Bills face off at 8:20 p.m. on NBC.

Wu-Tang Clan will perform in Detroit, Miranda Lambert in Texas, and the Goo Goo Dolls in New York. This doesn't include the first-ever Thanksgiving Eve game between the Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams, where U2 will perform at halftime.

Here is a look at 10 of the most memorable Thanksgiving halftime show performances:

You can't mention Thanksgiving halftime shows without mentioning Creed's Texas performance. There were silk aerial dancers, a man flying through with giant wings, mimes, choir singers and many other elements that left people with a lot of questions.

A 77-year-old Dolly Parton took the stage in a Dallas Cowgirl costume and proved why she is a country music legend. She performed "Jolene," "9 to 5" and made a medley with Queen's "We Will Rock You" and "We Are The Champions." Any Dolly performance is automatically iconic, and this was no different.

Since she kicked off the tradition of Thanksgiving halftime shows, McEntire has to make the list. She performed at Texas Stadium with a choir and dancers, along with the Cowboys cheerleaders. The country music legend performed on a star stage and wore Cowboys blue.

Any Destiny's Child performance is automatically memorable. The Texas natives were an obvious choice for a Texas halftime show. The trio performed "Lose My Breath" and "Soldier" from their final studio album. A marching band and cheerleaders joined their performance.

Mariah Carey may be the queen of Christmas, but she also knows how to put on a great Thanksgiving performance. The pop diva performed "Shake It Off" and "Don't Forget About Us" from her album The Emancipation of Mimi. The Ford Field performance also included a marching band and dancers. Carey began the performance being carried by men in football uniforms. People on the field created an "M" and "C."

Carrie Underwood has become an NFL staple, as the singer of Sunday Night Football's theme song. Her ties to the NFL also include a Thanksgiving halftime show performance. She sang her hit song "Before He Cheats" and "We're Young and Beautiful" in Texas, with an elaborate backdrop and silk aerial dancers.

Sheryl Crow brought down the Texas house with her rock vibes on Thanksgiving Day. She performed "Good Is Good" and her biggest hit "If It Makes You Happy." With acrobatic dancers and ribbon twirlers, her exciting performance became an instant classic.

The Jonas Brothers are one of the biggest boy bands of the early 2000s and 2010s, and their performance was a hit with any Disney Channel fans. The Jonas Brothers performed in 2008, singing hits including "Lovebug" and "Burnin Up." They became the first-ever repeat performers when they returned in 2022, singing new hits including "Only Human." Both performances were at the Cowboys' home.

The American Idol winner and Texas native performed in front of the Cowboys' home fans. She performed "Walk Away," "Never Again," and "Since U Been Gone" in front of Dallas cheerleaders, pyrotechnics, and flashing lights.

Jack White headlined last year's Lions Thanksgiving halftime show and surprised fans by bringing out Eminem. White opened with "That's How I'm Feeling" before introducing Eminem, who performed "Till I Collapse." The two Detroit natives were a hit with the Ford Field crowd.
